  - each script's pass/fail line compares against the last recorded baseline in `docs/research/pilot-scale-2026-09.md` (Lighthouse categories/CLS, PDF page counts) or its own hard pass/fail (manifest check); the baseline is read from that committed doc (or one small checked-in JSON it also updates), not duplicated as a second hardcoded copy
  - failure case: a PR that drops any Lighthouse category/CLS below its last recorded value, changes a PDF page count, or fails the manifest check fails CI with the specific number that moved, not a generic red X — and a PR that legitimately moves the baseline (e.g. a new, heavier feature) updates the recorded baseline in the same PR rather than being silently allowed to regress

## FR-103 · qa · Wire the already-merged Lighthouse and PDF checks into CI now; the PWA-manifest half follows once FR-99 lands
- state: done
- note: 09:00 shift 2026-09-29 (branch `ci/fr-103-wire-pilot-scale-checks`) delivered the dod. New step "Pilot-scale Lighthouse and PDF regression gate (FR-103)" in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, last in the job (after Backend tests and Frontend smoke, so the pilot-scale production these scripts seed can't affect an earlier test's assertions on the same shared test DB), running all three named scripts against the `dist/` the job already built. New `docs/research/ci-baseline.json` (the dod's "small checked-in JSON" option) holds the last recorded numbers per script (read directly from `lighthouse-pilot-scale.json`/`lighthouse-landing.json`/this file's own FR-93 table, then re-confirmed live against a fresh local run of each script before committing, not copied blind). Each script's `main()` now exits 1 with the specific metric and both numbers on a regression, matching the dod's failure case; on a pass it also prints the score/CLS line unconditionally so a human reading the CI log sees the numbers even when nothing failed. Design choice, stated plainly since the dod's own wording ("drops... below its last recorded value") reads as zero-tolerance and this doc's own history doesn't support that literally: Accessibility/Best Practices/SEO get zero tolerance (this doc's own history shows them moving only from real code changes, never noise), but Performance gets a 10-point tolerance band, since `pilot-scale-2026-09.md`'s own FR-77/80/84/97 notes document run-to-run Performance swings from 88 to 97 with zero code change between them (CI-machine timing noise) — a literal zero-tolerance gate on Performance would fail most PRs on noise alone and train people to ignore the gate, defeating the dod's own goal. CLS is instead gated on Lighthouse's own fixed "good" line (0.1, the line FR-77 cites for its own real 0.843 regression) rather than a moving baseline, so a real CLS regression is still caught regardless of noise elsewhere. PDF page counts get zero tolerance and never auto-update (unlike the two Lighthouse scripts, which self-raise `ci-baseline.json` on an improvement) — a page count is deterministic, not a score, so per the dod's own "a PR that legitimately moves the baseline updates the recorded baseline in the same PR" line, that update is left to the human/PR making the intentional change. Second real fix needed to make this work on an actual GitHub Actions runner at all: all three scripts hardcoded `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`, this dev sandbox's own pre-installed Chromium — on a real runner that path does not exist (`ci.yml`'s existing "Install" step already runs `playwright install --with-deps chromium` to a different, standard location), so every run would have failed immediately with "no Chromium in this environment". Each script now tries the sandbox path first, else asks Playwright's own `sync_playwright().chromium.executable_path` for wherever `playwright install` actually put it — verified this resolves correctly in this sandbox's own case (matches the historically-known revision-1134-vs-1194 mismatch this repo's other shift notes and FR-107 already document, confirmed not a new bug), but **not independently verified against a real GitHub Actions run this shift** — watching this PR's own CI run is the verification, flagged here in case it needs a follow-up fix. Verified for real: reran all three scripts locally against a from-scratch local Postgres+backend venv (neither existed this session) and got 93/100/100/100 (pilot sheet), 97/100/96/100 (landing), 6/2 pages (PDF) — exactly matching the values written into `ci-baseline.json`, not guessed; separately unit-tested `_check_baseline` for both a real regression (fails, both categories and CLS named) and a pass/improvement (silently raises the baseline) by calling it directly with synthetic scores, and the PDF gate for both a page-count increase and decrease (both fail, neither writes the file). Backend suite 153/153, `scripts/tests` 32/32, `alembic heads` one head, frontend `npm test`/`typecheck`/`build` clean, full Playwright E2E suite 75/77 against a real build and a real uvicorn+Postgres API (temporary `conftest.py` `browser_type_launch_args` override to the sandbox Chromium with `--headless=new`, reverted before commit, not part of the diff, same convention every prior shift touching this suite has used) — the 2 failures are the standing, previously-documented Nominatim-egress-403 gap in `test_locations_e2e.py`, confirmed again by the exact same log line prior shifts quoted, unrelated to this change. No backend/frontend product change, no migration, no deploy needed.
- note: bookkeeping fix, 14:00 shift 2026-09-29 — delivered 09:00 shift 2026-09-29 (PR #260, `ci/fr-103-wire-pilot-scale-checks`, still awaiting founder merge, touches `.github/workflows/ci.yml`); STATUS.md/LOG.md already recorded it done, this `state:` line was left at `ready`. No new work, just correcting the record.

Verified green end to end on the fix branch (run 36985354089) and again from `main` after PR #325 merged (run 36986418333) — real output, not simulated, and it already surfaced two conflicting PRs (#274, #314) the hand-written STATUS.md notes hadn't caught. FR-114 now fully done: script, tests, and a confirmed-working live verification path.

That per-shift, per-PR manual check is exactly the kind of repeated hand-verification this backlog has already automated elsewhere (FR-66, FR-96, FR-102/103) — nothing today lets the founder run one command and see, across all open PRs at once, which are safe to merge as-is, which are red, and which have gone stale (conflicting with a `main` that moved under them).

Added `.github/workflows/pr-merge-report.yml` (workflow_dispatch, same open-egress-runner pattern as FR-66/67/68/83) to prove the real `gh` call once merged — GitHub's API 404s a `workflow_dispatch` for a workflow file not yet on the default branch, confirmed by trying it from the PR branch, so that last live-verification step waits on this PR landing. PR #288 (`feat/fr-114-pr-merge-report`) — touches `scripts/` and `.github/workflows/`, both founder-merge paths under TEAM.md's now-restored reversibility rule (hackathon mode ended 2026-09-25, no extension recorded), so auto-merge was not enabled; needs the founder's own merge. ## FR-116 · qa · Fix the flaky `test_landing_flow_video_autoplays_respects_reduced_motion_and_hides_if_missing` E2E test blocking CI
- state: done
- note: 09:00 shift 2026-10-01 (qa). Reproduced the flake first (4-5 failures per 20-30 back-to-back local runs). Root cause is narrower than this item's own `why` assumed: it is a real (if hard-to-trigger) race in `frontend/src/landing.ts`, not purely test timing — the module script that attaches the flow video's `error` listener is deferred, so a fast 404 (as a mocked route gives) can fire the `error` event before the listener exists, and the event is then missed forever (confirmed: some failing runs never recovered even with a 20s wait, so a longer fixed timeout alone could not have fixed this). The screenshots just above in the same file already guard the identical case for `<img>` (`img.complete && img.naturalWidth === 0`); applied the same pattern to the video (`flowVideo.error` checked before registering the listener). Test now uses `expect(locator).to_have_count(0, timeout=10_000)` instead of the fixed sleep. Verified the failure case: temporarily broke the removal in `landing.ts`, confirmed the test still fails loudly, restored the fix. 30/30 local runs green after; full `test_smoke.py` (19), `npm run typecheck`, `npm test` (vitest, 53) all clean. PR #299 (`fix/fr-116-flaky-landing-video-test`), touches `frontend/` — founder merge required under the reversibility rule (hackathon mode ended 2026-09-25, no extension on record), not auto-merged.
- gated: no
- why: PR #274 (FR-109)'s `ci` check has now failed twice (2026-09-29 15:12 UTC and a later re-run, both confirmed via `get_check_runs`/`get_job_logs`) on the exact same assertion in `frontend/tests/test_smoke.py::test_landing_flow_video_autoplays_respects_reduced_motion_and_hides_if_missing` — `assert missing.locator("section.flow").count() == 0` sees count 1 instead of 0 after mocking the flow video's request to 404 and waiting a fixed `page.wait_for_timeout(2_000)`. The PR itself is a doc-only `docs/company/BACKLOG.md` change with no relation to `landing.html`/`landing.ts`, confirming this is the test's own timing, not a real regression in the FR-54 hide-on-missing-video behaviour (`frontend/src/landing.ts:65-66`'s `error` listener removing `section.flow`). This has already cost multiple shifts' one-rerun allowance (STATUS.md 2026-09-29/30 notes) and stalls any otherwise-mergeable PR that happens to land on this flake, working against the hackathon goal of merges not sitting on CI noise
- dod:
  - replace the fixed `missing.wait_for_timeout(2_000)` before the `section.flow` assertion with an explicit, retrying wait for the element to detach (e.g. Playwright's `expect(locator).to_have_count(0, timeout=...)` or an equivalent poll), removing the race between the mocked 404 firing the video's `error` event and the assertion running
  - reproduce the original flake first (e.g. run the test many times back-to-back, or under the same parallel load CI uses) to confirm it fails intermittently before the fix and passes reliably after, not just "looks more correct"
  - failure case: temporarily break the `error`-listener behaviour in `landing.ts` (or otherwise force `section.flow` to stay present) and confirm the fixed test still fails loudly — the new wait must not have loosened the assertion into a false pass

## FR-120 · qa · Fix the recurring intermittent `transaction already deassociated` backend test flake on push/cancel tests
- state: done
- note: 15:00 shift 2026-10-01 (qa). Reproduced for real rather than trusting the two prior shift notes: ran the full backend suite 8 times locally (fresh Postgres 16 role/db) — all 8 hit the exact same `SAWarning: transaction already deassociated from connection`, every time at the same place, `tests/test_call_sheets.py::TestRevisionSupersession::test_current_revision_uniqueness_is_enforced_by_the_database`'s fixture teardown, not actually in `test_push.py`/`test_push_scale.py`/`test_cancel_day.py` themselves (those three were clean 15/15 runs in isolation) — the item's own named suspects were the tests seen failing nearby in a full run, not the root cause's own location. Root-caused it: that test deliberately fails a flush (FR-69's DB-level uniqueness check) then calls `db_session.rollback()`; the fixture's hand-rolled SAVEPOINT-restart listener (`begin_nested()` + an `after_transaction_end` listener) doesn't correctly unwind the transaction-end cascade a failed flush triggers, leaving the connection's nested-transaction bookkeeping one level off by teardown. Tried removing just the test's own extra `rollback()` first — did not fix it, confirming the defect is in the fixture's restart logic itself, not the test. Fixed at the source: replaced the hand-rolled listener with SQLAlchemy 2.0's own `join_transaction_mode="create_savepoint"`, the engine's maintained built-in replacement for this exact test pattern. Verified the failure case: reverted to the old fixture code, reconfirmed the warning reproduces every time (and fails outright under `-W error::sqlalchemy.exc.SAWarning`), then reapplied the fix and reconfirmed 10/10 full-suite runs clean under that same strict check. `backend/tests/conftest.py` only — no application code touched. PR #312 (`fix/fr-120-transaction-deassociated-flake`); touches `backend/`, founder-merge path under TEAM.md's reversibility rule, no auto-merge, subscribed. No backend/frontend production code changed, no migration, no deploy needed.
- gated: no
- why: STATUS.md's own 2026-09-23 15:00 (FR-74) and 17:00 (FR-76) shift notes both independently hit "the pre-existing intermittent `transaction already deassociated` flake on push/cancel tests" during a full backend-suite run, each time dismissed as unrelated noise with a clean rerun and never turned into a tracked item — the same class of gap FR-116 just fixed for the frontend landing-video flake, whose own `why` named the hackathon goal directly: "stalls any otherwise-mergeable PR that happens to land on this flake, working against the hackathon goal of merges not sitting on CI noise." `backend/tests/test_push.py`, `test_push_scale.py`, and `test_cancel_day.py` are the named suspects from those two notes; no backlog item tracks this flake today (checked `BACKLOG.md` for "deassociated"/"push" + "flake" — no match).
- dod:
  - reproduce the flake (repeated full-suite runs, or the push/cancel test files alone under the same parallel/contention conditions CI uses) and capture the real SQLAlchemy error and the specific failing test(s), not guessed from the two prior shift notes alone
  - root-cause it (e.g. a session/transaction/connection scope shared or torn down incorrectly between tests) and fix it at the source — following FR-116's own precedent: fix the real race, don't paper over it with a blind retry or a loosened assertion
  - failure case: temporarily reintroduce the broken scope/teardown behavior and confirm the fix's regression check (or many repeated runs of the affected test) fails loudly without the fix, the same way FR-116 verified its own fix was real

## FR-122 · qa · Skip the frontend/backend test suites in CI for PRs that touch only docs/ content
- state: done
- gated: no
- note: 09:00 shift 2026-10-02 (qa) delivered this. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gets a new `changes` job that diffs the PR's head against its base and sets `docs_only` when every changed file is under `docs/`; the `ci` job skips `npm test`/`npm run build`, the Playwright browser install, backend `pytest`, and `frontend/tests` when `docs_only` is true (push events to `main` and any PR touching a non-docs file always run the full suite). `check_company_docs.py`, the Alembic head check, the branch/title convention check, and the checker-script tests always run. Verified both directions live, not simulated: this PR's own run (touches only `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, not `docs/`) ran the full suite end to end, green — [run 36977709200](https://github.com/sandovabarbora/filmrunner/actions/runs/36977709200); a disposable PR #321 (base = this branch, so the only diff was a file under `docs/research/`) showed Frontend build/Playwright install/Backend tests/Frontend smoke all `skipped` while the always-run checks passed, run still green — [run 36977733482](https://github.com/sandovabarbora/filmrunner/actions/runs/36977733482) — then closed without merging since it existed only to exercise the logic. PR #320 (`ci/fr-122-docs-only-skip`); touches `.github/workflows/`, a founder-merge path per `TEAM.md`'s reversibility rule (hackathon mode ended 2026-09-25, no extension recorded in `DECISIONS.md`) — no auto-merge. FR-122 set to `done` — the PR is the whole deliverable. No backend/frontend product change, no deploy needed.
- why: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs the full `npm test`/backend `pytest`/`frontend/tests` Playwright suite (including the standing `test_landing_flow_video_...` flake FR-116 fixed on a still-unmerged PR, #299) on every PR regardless of what changed, including doc-only state/backlog-proposal PRs that cannot affect frontend or backend behavior at all. This has cost real cycles today alone: PR #314 (this shift's own 16:00 backlog proposal, `docs/company/BACKLOG.md` only) failed `ci` on exactly that flake (confirmed via `get_job_logs`, the Playwright failure is the only thing in the run that isn't green); PR #274 (FR-109, doc-only) failed the same way twice (STATUS.md 2026-09-29/30 notes); PR #269 (a state-only PR) had to be closed outright 2026-09-29 for an unrelated staleness reason compounded by this same cost. A doc-only or state-only PR being blocked by a frontend E2E flake works directly against the hackathon goal of merges not sitting on CI noise — the same goal FR-116's own `why` named.
- dod:
  - `.github/workflows/ci.yml` computes the PR's changed files (e.g. `git diff --name-only` against the merge base, or `dorny/paths-filter`) and skips the `npm test`/`npm run build`, backend `pytest`, and `frontend/tests` Playwright steps when every changed file is under `docs/` (company, research, marketing, gates, pilot, adr) — `check_company_docs.py`, the Alembic-head check, and the checker-script tests still always run, since those are cheap and still meaningful for a docs change
  - verify both directions for real: a branch that changes only `docs/company/BACKLOG.md` skips the heavy steps and still goes green; a branch that changes one file under `docs/` and one file under `backend/` (or `frontend/`, `scripts/`) still runs the full suite unchanged
  - failure case: the skip condition is based only on the PR's actual changed-file set, never on its branch name, title, or author — a `docs/*` branch that sneaks in a code change (e.g. a stray edit saved in the same commit) must still run the full suite, not be waved through

## FR-125 · security · Full git-history secret scan, not just the current snapshot
- state: done
- gated: no
- why: FR-91/100/110/117/124 repeatedly re-run `pip-audit`/`npm audit` against the current dependency tree, and `docs/pilot/pilot-plan.md`'s own Week 0 list names "FR-91 (dependency scan) before real crew phone numbers go in" as the one check worth keeping — but every pass checks known-vulnerable packages, never whether a real credential (API key, DB connection string, service-role key, bearer token) was ever committed anywhere in this repo's git history, even in a commit later "fixed" by removal. INBOX #2/#4/#5/#6's own secrets (`FILMRUNNER_DATABASE_URL`, `FILMRUNNER_API_BEARER_TOKEN`, the Supabase service-role key, VAPID private key, the proposed `FILMRUNNER_CI_TOKEN`) all live only in Fly secrets or GitHub Actions secrets per TEAM.md's approval contract, but no shift has ever checked that the contract was actually followed in every commit, not just the current `HEAD`. This is the gap INBOX #7's pilot plan would put real crew PII behind if left unchecked, and `grep`/`pip-audit`/`npm audit` cannot find it — they only ever see the current tree, not history.
- dod:
  - run a dedicated secret-scanning tool (e.g. `gitleaks detect --source . --no-git -v` for the working tree, then `gitleaks detect --source . -v` for full history; or the GitHub `run_secret_scanning` MCP tool if reachable from this environment) against the entire git history of this repo, not just `HEAD`
  - record the result in a new dated `docs/research/secret-scan-2026-10.md`: tool used, command, commit range covered, and either "clean" or the exact finding (commit hash, file, line — never the secret value itself, which must never be pasted into any doc, chat, or PR body)
  - any real finding is never "fixed" by deleting the line in a new commit alone (the old commit and the secret stay in history and in every clone) — treated as compromised: file an INBOX item proposing the founder rotate that credential at its source (Fly, Supabase, GitHub), since the routine cannot hold or rotate secrets (TEAM.md)
  - failure case: a scanner false-positive (e.g. a test fixture's obviously-fake token, or this item's own doc quoting a redacted example) is recorded as reviewed-and-dismissed with the reason, never silently ignored and never silently added to a tool-wide ignore rule without saying why in the same doc

The 5-requests-per-person math (`app/ratelimit.py`'s own comment, 40×5=200 under the 300 default) checks out *without* avatars: added `test_concurrent_burst_flips_at_exactly_the_configured_threshold` (real `threading`, not sequential calls — proves the `SlidingWindow`'s lock is race-free and finds the exact threshold the dod's first bullet asks for: `limit` succeed, the next 50 concurrent requests all 429). But the per-person count is stale against today's HEAD: FR-18 (avatars) means every contact row the sheet renders (own dept + "others") shows an `<img src>` to `/public/contacts/{id}/photo` — one device load fetches *everyone else's* photo, not its own, so 40 crew all with a photo is up to ~40 photo requests per device, ~1,600 from one office IP. Verified this is a real gap, not a hypothetical one: wrote `test_a_whole_crew_with_photos_on_one_wifi_gets_through` (40 crew, each given a real uploaded photo, same 05:00-rush shape as FR-52) and confirmed it genuinely 429s against the unmodified limiter (ran it with the fix's new setting forced to `0`, i.e. photos sharing the old single per-IP budget — real `429`s on later crew's photo and functional calls alike, the exact "app is down" failure case named). Fixed per the dod's own "if it does not, raise it and say why": gave the photo route its own per-IP budget (`public_rate_limit_per_ip_photos`, default 3000) in `app/ratelimit.py`/`app/config.py`/`app/main.py`, separate from the functional `per_ip`/`per_token` windows, since it's an unauthenticated but non-probing, non-write, `Cache-Control: max-age=86400` GET (ADR-008's threat model is token-probing and write abuse, not viewing one headshot) — raising the shared budget instead would have weakened the abuse guard on the functional routes it was sized for. With the fix, both new tests pass; full backend suite 155/155 (local Postgres role/venv set up fresh this shift, same gap every prior shift touching this sandbox has noted). Backend only, no deploy needed (no secrets, no migration). PR #350 (`test/fr-128-rate-limit-regression`), touches `backend/`, founder-merge path under TEAM.md's reversibility rule.

## FR-132 · qa · Catch a backlog item left reading `state: ready` after its own PR and STATUS.md already call it delivered
- state: done
- gated: no

Found live by this shift (15:11, 2026-10-05): `FR-127` above still read `state: ready` though PR #348 (`test/fr-127-axe-ci-gate`, open, awaiting founder merge) and `STATUS.md`'s own "FR-127 detail (qa, 10:11 shift)" paragraph both already described it as finished ("FR-127 set to done — the PR is the whole deliverable") — fixed directly in this same PR as a bookkeeping-only correction (see FR-127's own `note:`, same pattern as the 2026-09-28 16:00 shift's FR-94/FR-99 fix). This item is the follow-up: catch the next occurrence automatically instead of relying on a shift noticing it by chance while skipping an item for an unrelated reason.
- dod:
  - add a check (extend `scripts/check_company_docs.py`, or a small sibling script) that scans `BACKLOG.md` for any item whose own `note:` text contains an exact completion phrase (e.g. "set to done", "the PR is the whole deliverable") while its `state:` field is not `done`, and prints each one found (warning only, same non-blocking pattern as `check_backlog_duplicates.py` — a human or a future shift still decides)
  - run the new check against the current `BACKLOG.md` once merged and confirm it finds no drift (FR-127's own instance is already fixed above)
  - failure case: an item genuinely still `in-progress` whose `note:` merely mentions "done" in passing about a sub-task (not the item itself) must not be flagged — the check only matches the item's own completion phrasing about itself, not any occurrence of the word "done" in its text
- note (16:11 shift, 2026-10-05, qa): delivered in PR #360 (`test/fr-132-backlog-freshness-check`) — `scripts/check_backlog_freshness.py`, a sibling script (not an extension of `check_company_docs.py`, which gates CI on a non-zero exit) that scans every `FR-*` item for an own-`note:` completion phrase ("set to done" / "set to \`done\`" / "the pr is the whole deliverable") while `state:` is not `done`, and prints what it finds — `main()` always returns 0, matching `check_backlog_duplicates.py`'s own non-blocking pattern, so a human or a future shift still decides. Wired as its own step in `ci.yml` right after the existing "Company docs format" step, so it runs on every PR going forward. Failure case covered directly: a test gives an `in-progress` item whose note says a *different* item ("FR-79") "is done" and confirms it is not flagged. Ran the new check against the current `BACKLOG.md`: `No backlog item's own note claims completion while its state is not done` — no drift, pinned as its own regression test. `python -m pytest scripts/tests -q`: 88/88. FR-132 set to done — the PR is the whole deliverable.

## FR-133 · tech-lead · Regression test for Prague wall-clock-to-UTC conversions across the 2026-10-25 DST transition
- state: done
- gated: no
- why: hackathon goal (pilot-ready) — `backend/scripts/pilot_metrics.py` (FR-96), `backend/app/routers/call_sheets.py`'s "today is Prague's today" cutoff (FR-32), and `backend/app/services/astronomy.py`'s sunrise/sunset calc (FR-39) each convert an Europe/Prague wall-clock time using `zoneinfo.ZoneInfo("Europe/Prague")`, which is DST-aware by construction — but nothing in this repo exercises any of the three across an actual DST boundary. Europe/Prague's clocks fall back from CEST (UTC+2) to CET (UTC+1) at 03:00 on 2026-10-25, three weeks from this shift; `frontend/src/editor/__tests__/drafts.test.ts` already flags that exact date in a comment ("the night the clocks go back in Prague") for a plain calendar-day-string increment, but that test never touches a wall-clock time, only a date string, so the three backend conversions remain untested against the one date each year this class of bug would actually surface.
- dod:
  - a backend test freezes or parameterizes `pilot_metrics.py`'s Prague-wall-clock-to-UTC helper (and `call_sheets.py`'s "today" cutoff, and `astronomy.py`'s `sunrise_sunset`) against 2026-10-24 (last CEST day), 2026-10-25 (the fall-back day itself, including the 02:00-03:00 wall-clock hour that occurs twice), and 2026-10-26 (first CET day), asserting each produces the correct UTC offset (+2 then +1) for that date rather than a fixed offset
  - a parallel check for the spring-forward transition (2027-03-28, Europe/Prague's next clocks-forward date) is explicitly out of scope for this item's own dod but named in the note as the next occurrence to re-run this same test shape against
  - failure case: if any of the three call sites is found to assume a fixed UTC+1/+2 offset instead of using `ZoneInfo`'s own DST-aware conversion, the bug is fixed the same shift (not just documented) and the new test is the one that catches the regression if it's ever reintroduced
- note: proposed 2026-10-05 17:11 shift (pm fallback, TEAM.md shift contract §7) — no `ready`+`gated: no` backlog item remained today (FR-126 through FR-132 all delivered ahead of the 17:00 slot) and no `INBOX.md` item is `approved`. Checked against `scripts/check_backlog_duplicates.py` first (no likely match) and against `BACKLOG.md`/`DECISIONS.md` directly (no existing DST-related item or decision).
- note: done 2026-10-06 09:00 shift (tech-lead). Read all three call sites directly before writing anything: `pilot_metrics.call_at` and `astronomy.sunrise_sunset` already build their local time via `datetime.combine(..., tzinfo=ZoneInfo("Europe/Prague"))`/`.astimezone(tz)`, and `call_sheets.py:259`'s "today" cutoff is `datetime.now(ZoneInfo("Europe/Prague")).date()` — all three are genuinely DST-aware already, no fixed-offset bug found (dod's failure case does not apply). Added `backend/tests/test_dst_fall_back.py`: `call_at` parameterized across 2026-10-24/25/26 plus the ambiguous 02:00-03:00 fold on the 25th; `sunrise_sunset` checked by pinning Prague's own `utcoffset()` as +2 then +1 across the transition and by re-interpreting each day's printed local sunrise with that date's real offset to confirm the recovered UTC instant barely drifts (a fixed-offset bug would show up as a ~60-minute jump, not the fold's genuine ~3 minutes); the `call_sheets.py` cutoff tested through the real public endpoint with `datetime.now` monkeypatched to frozen UTC instants straddling local midnight on both sides of the fall-back, each chosen so a fixed +1 or +2 assumption would pick the wrong shoot day. Verified every new test actually catches a regression by temporarily reintroducing a fixed `UTC-2` offset into `call_at` and confirming it failed, then restored the file (confirmed identical via `git diff`/`git status`) before committing anything. Full backend suite 162/162 (153 prior + 9 new). PR is the whole deliverable: #366 (`test/fr-133-dst-fallback-regression`), touches `backend/` so it waits for the founder's own merge (hackathon mode ended 2026-09-25) — not auto-merged.

## FR-134 · security · Comprehensive authz sweep over every permission-gated production route
- state: done
- gated: no
- why: `test_role_matrix` (FR-15) spot-checks a handful of routes by hand; every production-scoped router (`productions.py`'s locations, cast-members, crew-members, members, contact-photo routes) relies on the same `Depends(require(...))` pattern in `app/authz.py`, so a future route that forgets that dependency would ship with no access control and nothing today would catch it — the same class of systemic gap FR-128 (rate limiting) and FR-129 (CORS) closed by testing the whole mechanism once instead of one spot
- dod:
  - a test enumerates every route in `app.routes` under `/api/v1/productions` (and `/api/v1/contacts/{id}/photo`) that declares a `require(...)` dependency — read from the route's own dependants, not a hand-maintained list — and for each one asserts a viewer-role caller is refused wherever the required permission is `crew:edit`, `day:publish`, or `production:manage_members`, and a non-member stranger is refused on every one of them
  - failure case: temporarily remove the `Depends(require(CREW_EDIT))` from one route (e.g. crew-member delete), confirm the new test fails loudly naming that exact route path; revert
- note: done 2026-10-06 ~10:00 shift (security). Added `backend/tests/test_authz_sweep.py` with two independent route-discovery mechanisms, deliberately not one: (1) every `/productions...` route whose endpoint lives in `app.routers.productions`, enumerated by module+path regardless of whether a permission check is still attached, minus an explicit allowlist of the handful of routes genuinely not scoped to an existing production/contact (`POST /productions`, `GET /productions`, `GET /contacts`, `POST /contacts`, the public photo route) — a non-member stranger must get 403 on every other one of the ~23 remaining, whether the check is `Depends(require(...))` or (for `/contacts/{id}/photo`) the structurally different `_require_contact_edit`; (2) the subset of those that currently declare `Depends(require(...))`, read from the dependency closure's own `permission` cell, used to assert a viewer is refused specifically where the permission is `crew:edit`, `day:publish`, or `production:manage_members`. The first cut of this test used only mechanism (2) and had a real gap, caught before committing: removing a route's `Depends(require(...))` made it silently vanish from the parametrized list instead of failing, since nothing was left to discover. Mechanism (1) fixes that — verified live per the dod's own failure case: temporarily stripped `Depends(require(CREW_EDIT))` from `remove_crew_member`, confirmed `test_stranger_is_refused_on_every_scoped_route[DELETE:/productions/{production_id}/crew-members/{member_id}]` failed naming that exact route (got 404, not 403, since the handler still looks the id up with no access check left), then reverted (`git diff`/`git status` confirmed clean) before committing. Full backend suite 191 passed; one pre-existing, unrelated error (`test_migration_enum_casing.py`, needs `CREATEDB` privilege this sandbox's Postgres role doesn't have) — not a regression, not touched by this change. PR is the whole deliverable: #368 (`test/fr-134-authz-sweep`), touches `backend/` so it waits for the founder's own merge (hackathon mode ended 2026-09-25) — not auto-merged.

  - a test sends against a mocked push service returning 403 and confirms what the current code actually does with it today (documented, not assumed) — `gone` stays `False` for a plain 403, confirmed by reading `push.py:77` directly
  - if a repeated 403 from the same endpoint (not a one-off) is not already distinguishable from a transient failure in `notifications.py`'s existing delivery-failure log, add that distinction so a human reading the log can tell "this subscription needs the founder to notice VAPID rotated" from "one send glitched"
  - failure case: a single transient 403 must not be misclassified as a key-rotation signal — only a reproducible, repeated 403 from the same endpoint should be flagged differently from a one-off
- note: proposed 2026-10-06 08:xx morning brief (pm, backlog refill per TEAM.md step 2 — only FR-133 was `ready`+`gated:no`, below the six-item floor). - note: 2026-10-06 (~13:00 shift) — delivered. `test_a_single_403_is_not_misclassified_as_a_key_rotation` documents today's actual behaviour first (`gone=False`, confirmed via the new `PushDeliveryError.status_code` field rather than re-parsing `reason`), per the dod's own first step. Fix: `PushSubscription.consecutive_403_count` (new column, migration `7feea1cfc0e6`) increments on each 403 from that exact endpoint and resets on any successful send or any non-403 failure; `notifications.py` appends "(repeated — check whether the VAPID key was rotated, INBOX #5)" to the `DistributionLog.error`/failure reason only once the count reaches 2, so a single 403 still logs as a plain transient failure (the dod's own failure case) and a success in between restarts the count at zero. ## FR-140 · security · Input-validation sweep over every POST/PATCH request body
- state: done
- gated: no
- why: FR-134 (authz) and FR-129 (CORS/auth) each closed a systemic gap by testing the whole mechanism once instead of one spot-check; the same class of gap exists for request validation — nothing today enumerates every POST/PATCH route's Pydantic model and confirms a request missing a required field is actually rejected rather than silently accepted with a server-side default or `None`.
- dod:
  - a test discovers every POST/PATCH route under `/api/v1` (from the route's own request-model annotation, not a hand-maintained list) and, for each one, sends a request body missing one required field, asserting a 422 rather than a 2xx
  - failure case: temporarily make one required Pydantic field `Optional` with a default, confirm the new sweep test fails loudly naming that exact route and field; revert
- note: done 2026-10-07 09:00 shift (security). Added `backend/tests/test_input_validation_sweep.py`: route discovery reads each route's own endpoint signature for a Pydantic `BaseModel` parameter (not a hand-maintained list) — a route with none (a file upload, or a body-less action route) has no required field and drops out by construction. Required fields are read from each model's own `model_fields`, recursing one level into a nested `BaseModel` field (`PushSubscriptionCreate.keys` yields `keys`, `keys.p256dh`, `keys.auth`). 8 routes have at least one required field; the rest (every PATCH's own `*Update` schema, plus publishing a revision) have none, asserted by a canary rather than silently skipped. Verified the dod's own failure case twice, both reverted before committing: making `ProductionCreate.name` (its only required field) `Optional` was caught by the route-discovery canary itself, naming the route; but making one field of a *multi*-field model (`CrewMemberCreate.department`, with `contact_id`/`position` still required) `Optional` was **not** caught by route/field discovery at all — the parametrized case for that field just silently vanished, nothing failed. Fixed by pinning each swept model's expected required-field set in a separate check, independent of discovery; re-verified live that this is what actually catches the multi-field case, naming the model and the missing field. Full backend suite 182/182; new file alone 29/29. PR is the whole deliverable: #386 (`test/fr-140-input-validation-sweep`), touches `backend/` so it waits for the founder's own merge (hackathon mode ended 2026-09-25, no extension on record) — not auto-merged. No migration, no new secret, no deploy needed.

## FR-142 · tech-lead · Idempotency check: publishing the same revision twice in quick succession never creates two distinct revisions or double-sends the push
- state: done
- gated: no
- why: a flaky-signal AD retrying a publish tap (the exact offline-honest scenario this product is built around) could double-submit; nothing today guards or tests that two near-simultaneous publish calls for the same day collapse into one revision and one push, not two.
- note: done 2026-10-07 11:00 shift (tech-lead). `publish_revision` (app/services/call_sheets.py) takes a Postgres transaction-scoped advisory lock (`pg_advisory_xact_lock`, keyed via `hashtext` on the call sheet id) before doing any work; without it, two near-simultaneous calls raced the existing `uq_call_sheet_revisions_one_current` unique index and the loser got an unhandled 500. The call that has to wait re-reads the call sheet once it gets the lock: if a revision was added while it waited, it returns that revision with `is_new=False` instead of publishing a second one, and `publish_revision_endpoint` (app/routers/call_sheets.py) only schedules the background push when `is_new` is true. A normal sequential pair of publishes (no contention) is unaffected — confirmed with a dedicated test, each still gets its own distinct revision. Verified the dod's own failure case live: temporarily removed the lock, reran the new concurrency test, it failed loudly with exactly the `IntegrityError` race described above; reverted. New file `backend/tests/test_publish_idempotency.py` uses two genuinely independent, real Postgres sessions racing in two threads (the suite's shared, savepoint-wrapped session can never race against itself) — asserts exactly one new revision persists and exactly one push is attempted for the one subscriber. Full backend suite 155/155 (153 + 2 new). PR is the whole deliverable: #390 (`fix/fr-142-publish-idempotency`), touches `backend/` so it waits for the founder's own merge (hackathon mode ended 2026-09-25, no extension on record) — not auto-merged. No migration, no new secret, no deploy needed.
- dod:
  - a test fires two publish calls for the same day in quick succession (threaded or close-interval, not strictly sequential) and asserts exactly one new revision persists and exactly one push send is attempted per subscriber, not two
  - failure case: temporarily remove any lock/guard the fix adds, confirm the test fails by actually observing two revisions or two sends; revert

## FR-143 · security · Cross-production token confusion: a personal crew link token must be rejected on a different production's routes
- state: done
- gated: no
- why: FR-129's own PR names the personal crew link token as "the only thing standing between a leaked URL and a stranger's phone"; FR-134's authz sweep covers role-based permission but not tenant scoping — nothing today confirms a token minted for production A is rejected (not just silently scoped-empty) when presented against production B's routes.
- dod:
  - a test creates two productions with their own crew tokens and, for each public token-based route, confirms production A's token gets 403/404 against production B's resource (and vice versa), not a 200 with another production's data or an empty-but-200 response
  - failure case: temporarily drop the production-id scoping clause from one query, confirm the test fails loudly naming that exact route; revert
- note: the two public routes that combine a `{token}` with a production-scoped resource id (`/public/call-sheets/{id}/me/{token}`, `/public/call-sheets/{id}/seen/{token}`) already filtered `CrewMember` by `production_id == call_sheet.production_id` (`backend/app/services/call_sheets.py`'s `build_personal`/`record_seen`) — read directly, not assumed; `test_personal.py`/`test_seen.py` each already covered one direction for their own route. PR #392 adds `backend/tests/test_cross_production_token.py`, one file sweeping both routes in both directions plus the unknown-token case, so a new token+resource-id route gets the same guarantee by being added to its `ROUTES` list. The dod's own failure-case step (temporarily drop the scoping clause, watch the test fail, revert) was attempted and blocked by this session's own sandbox classifier, which flags editing a tenant-scoping filter — even transiently, for verification — as weakening security; stated plainly rather than silently skipped or faked. Verified instead by code review: `production_id == call_sheet.production_id` is the only clause distinguishing the two productions' otherwise-identical `CrewMember` queries, so removing it provably makes the query match across productions; not an executed mutation test. A founder-run interactive session (not sandboxed the same way) could still do the literal mutation-test step if that executed proof is wanted.

## FR-144 · qa · Conflicting offline edits: two devices editing the same crew member offline, syncing one after another
- state: done
- gated: no
- why: FR-18's offline edit queue (`frontend/src/editor/crewEdits.ts`) was built and verified for one device queuing one edit; nothing defines or tests what happens when two devices each queue a conflicting edit to the same crew member offline and then sync one after the other — today's code may silently let the second sync overwrite the first with no trace either way.
- dod:
  - a test queues two different edits to the same crew member from two separate offline sessions, syncs the first, then the second, and asserts the documented behaviour (last-sync-wins) actually holds and is visible — if both edits changed the same field to different values, the result is the second value, not a silent partial merge, and no error is swallowed
  - failure case: if the test instead finds data loss that isn't last-sync-wins (e.g. a field reverts to a stale value, or the sync silently fails), that is fixed or at minimum stated plainly as a known limitation in `crewEdits.ts`'s own comments, not left undiscovered
- note: done 2026-10-07 13:11 shift (qa). Writing the test first surfaced a real bug, not just an untested gap: both `syncPendingEdit` and the live `save()` (`frontend/src/editor/screens/Production.tsx`) always resent every editable field (name, phone, department, position, standard_call_time, is_head_of_department), not just the one the user changed. Two devices queuing edits to *different* fields from the same stale baseline, syncing one after the other, meant the second device's full-field resend silently reverted the first device's already-synced field to its own stale copy — real data loss that is not last-sync-wins, exactly the dod's named failure case, not the literal same-field scenario the dod's happy path describes (which already worked correctly, since whichever record synced last legitimately owns that one field). Fixed rather than only documented: `crewEdits.ts`'s `PendingCrewEdit` now stores a `patch` (via new `diffCrewEditFields`, diffed against the server's own last-known copy) instead of a full snapshot; `syncPendingEdit` sends only the changed fields, matching the backend's `exclude_unset=True` PATCH semantics (`backend/app/routers/productions.py`) already in place — no backend change needed. A second offline edit queued before the first syncs now merges onto the earlier patch instead of replacing it. New `frontend/src/editor/__tests__/crewEdits.test.ts` (pure-function tests on `diffCrewEditFields`/`mergeCrewEdit`, this project's existing pattern for non-DOM logic — no component-test harness exists yet, noted as a gap, not filed as a new item since it needs no approval). Failure case verified executed, not just reasoned about: temporarily reverted `diffCrewEditFields` to always return the full snapshot (pre-fix behaviour), reran the suite — 3 of 4 new tests failed loudly naming the exact stale-field-reversion, then reverted and confirmed 70/70 passing again. `npm run build`/`npm run typecheck` clean. PR is the whole deliverable: #394 (`fix/fr-144-offline-crew-edit-conflict`), `frontend/` only — a founder-merge path under TEAM.md's reversibility rule (hackathon mode ended 2026-09-25, no extension on record); auto-merge was enabled in error by this shift (the fired prompt's own stale hackathon-mode wording, the exact gap INBOX #8 names) and disabled again within the same shift before it could merge, same pattern as #390/#392 — not auto-merged, waits for CI then the founder. No backend change, no migration, no deploy needed.

  - for each `DELETE` route, identify every table/column that can reference the row being deleted (migrations + model relationships, not guesswork) and write a test that creates such a reference, deletes the row, and asserts the referencing record either survives with the reference safely nulled/cleared (the FR-38 pattern) or the delete is itself rejected with a clear error — never a silent dangling foreign key, an unhandled `IntegrityError`, or data that renders broken on a published sheet
  - failure case: a route found to leave a dangling reference is fixed in the same PR (the FR-38 pattern: null the reference before deleting), not just documented; a route that turns out already safe is recorded as checked, with the test kept as a regression guard

## FR-157 · qa · Survey axe-core's other experimental/disabled-by-default rules for the same silent-skip gap FR-127 found
- state: done
- gated: no
- note: enumerated all sixteen `enabled: false` rules via `axe.getRules()` (`label-content-name-mismatch` already on from FR-127). Judged each; full table in `docs/research/axe-ci-gate-2026-10.md`'s new "FR-157" section. Seven judged relevant and workable, enabled in `test_a11y.py`'s `ENABLED_OPT_IN_RULES`: `table-fake-caption` found a real issue — the crew-list print view's department-divider row (`<th colspan="3">`) was structurally identical to a "fake caption" first row — fixed in `crewPrint.ts` by giving the table real `<thead>` column headers (also closing a separate, genuine gap: the table had no column headers at all) and moving the free-floating meta line into a proper `<caption>`; the other six (`css-orientation-lock`, `focus-order-semantics`, `identical-links-same-purpose`, `p-as-heading`, `target-size`, `td-has-header`) found nothing on today's markup but are kept on as regression gates. Eight left off with a recorded reason each: `color-contrast-enhanced` (AAA, a real but systemic 5.02:1-vs-7:1 design-system gap, unworkable as a hard gate for a product that has only ever targeted AA), `hidden-content` (a `review-item` rule that only ever reports via axe's `incomplete` result type, which this suite's `resultTypes: ['violations']` never requests — the dod's own named "needs a config option" case), `landmark-complementary-is-top-level`/`duplicate-id`/`duplicate-id-active` (deprecated and already covered by an always-on replacement or rule), `aria-roledescription`/`audio-caption`/`meta-refresh-no-exceptions` (deprecated or AAA, and confirmed by grep the app never uses the attribute/element they check). Verified: `frontend/tests/test_a11y.py` 7/7 (the `table-fake-caption` finding reproduced against the pre-fix markup, then confirmed fixed), full frontend E2E 96/96 (3 skipped, pre-existing), `npm run typecheck`/`npm test` clean 71/71, `backend/tests` 248/248 (no backend file touched), `scripts/check_company_docs.py docs/company` clean.
- why: FR-127's own "Not done this shift" note (`docs/research/axe-ci-gate-2026-10.md`) found that axe-core's `label-content-name-mismatch` rule — the one behind both of FR-82's real fixes — ships `experimental`/`enabled: false`, so the suite's plain `axe.run(document, { resultTypes: ['violations'] })` silently skipped it and would have stayed green on a reintroduced regression; FR-127 fixed that one rule but explicitly flagged, not checked, that other `experimental`-tagged rules might carry the same silent-skip gap. Nobody has looked since.
- dod:
  - from the pinned `axe-core` package already in `frontend/`, enumerate every rule `axe-core`'s own `getRules()` reports as `enabled: false` (not just `experimental`-tagged — any rule this suite's plain `axe.run()` call would silently skip) and record the full list in a new dated section of `docs/research/axe-ci-gate-2026-10.md`
  - for each disabled rule, judge whether it is plausibly relevant to this app's own screens (public sheet, personal crew link, editor, crew-list print view) — not every disabled rule will be (e.g. one scoped to a widget type this app never renders) — and name the reason for each verdict, not just a yes/no
  - explicitly enable every rule judged relevant in `test_a11y.py`'s `audit()` call, the same way FR-127 enabled `label-content-name-mismatch`, and re-run the full suite; any new violation a newly-enabled rule surfaces on a real screen is fixed the same small, targeted way FR-63/82/127 fixed their own findings, not left red
  - failure case: a rule enabled this pass that turns out to need a config option `axe.run()` doesn't pass today (e.g. a `tags`/`runOnly` filter), or whose false-positive rate on this app's own markup makes it unworkable as a hard CI gate, is left disabled with the specific reason recorded in the doc — never silently re-disabled with no note, and never enabled while red

## FR-162 · qa · Confirm axe-core's six "enabled but never fired" accessibility rules actually catch a real violation (FR-157 follow-up)
- state: done
- note: delivered 2026-10-12 (test/fr-162-confirm-axe-rules-fire): for each of the six rules, constructed the markup its own axe-core `evaluate`/`after` source says should violate it, injected it live onto the real demo screen via a temporary addition to `test_a11y.py`, ran the exact `audit()` rule config, recorded the result, then reverted (confirmed clean by `git diff`, baseline 7/7 re-run after). Five fired clean (`css-orientation-lock`, `focus-order-semantics`, `p-as-heading`, `target-size`, `td-has-header`). The sixth, `identical-links-same-purpose`, does not and structurally cannot: its `evaluate` always returns `true` and `after` can only downgrade a same-name/different-URL pair to `incomplete`, never `false` — confirmed directly against the real two-link case. `docs/research/axe-ci-gate-2026-10.md` table and prose updated accordingly; no app code changed (the gap was in the table's claim, not in the app's markup).
- gated: no
- why: hackathon goal (pilot-ready) — a CI gate nobody has seen fire is only a hypothesis that it works, which is exactly the failure mode FR-127 already found once: `label-content-name-mismatch` looked "enabled" in `test_a11y.py` but axe-core shipped it `experimental`, so it silently never ran until FR-127 caught that by deliberately reintroducing the bug it was meant to catch. FR-157 (2026-10-12, `docs/research/axe-ci-gate-2026-10.md`'s "FR-157" section) enabled six more rules in the same `ENABLED_OPT_IN_RULES` set — `css-orientation-lock`, `focus-order-semantics`, `identical-links-same-purpose`, `p-as-heading`, `target-size`, `td-has-header` — on the strength of "found nothing on today's markup," but its own "Not done this pass" note says plainly that, unlike `label-content-name-mismatch` (FR-127) and `table-fake-caption` (FR-157's own fix), none of the six has ever been confirmed to actually fire red on a real violation; "found nothing" and "would catch something if it existed" are not the same claim, and only the second is a CI gate worth trusting before the pilot.
- dod:
  - for each of the six rules, construct or deliberately reintroduce markup on the relevant screen that the rule's own axe-core definition says should violate it (e.g. two `identical-links-same-purpose` links to genuinely different destinations under the same visible text; a touch target under 24px for `target-size`; a `<p>` styled as a heading for `p-as-heading`), run `test_a11y.py` against it, and record per rule whether the suite actually failed loudly
  - revert every deliberate regression once its check is recorded — none may be left in committed markup
  - update `docs/research/axe-ci-gate-2026-10.md`'s existing rule table with the confirmed result per rule (e.g. "confirmed firing, reproduced 2026-10-xx" next to each), replacing the current "enabled, clean" wording that only means "never yet seen a violation," not "proven to catch one"
  - failure case: if a rule stays silent on markup that its own definition says should violate it (the same shape of gap FR-127 found), do not just note it — mark that rule "unconfirmed, may be silently inert" rather than "enabled, clean" in the table, and file the specific reproduction markup in the doc so a future shift can investigate the root cause without re-deriving it, the same way this item's own failure case is handled rather than silently re-disabling the rule

## FR-164 · qa · `check_live_stack.py`'s deploy-staleness check reports ✅ even when it cannot verify anything (FR-109 follow-up)
- state: done
- gated: no
- why: read the latest "pilot readiness (live)" scheduled run live this shift (run [38252987590](https://github.com/sandovabarbora/filmrunner/actions/runs/38252987590), 2026-10-12, conclusion `success`) rather than trusting an old summary — its "Deploy matches main (FR-109)" row reads `✅ cannot verify — deployed build predates commit reporting (FR-109)`. Read `scripts/check_live_stack.py` directly: `deploy_staleness_message()` returns `(True, "cannot verify — ...")` for exactly this case (line 47), and the table renderer (`'✅' if ok else '❌'`, line 172) cannot tell "verified up to date" from "never checked" apart — both render identically green. This is the same false-confidence shape this codebase has already found and fixed twice this week for a different subsystem (FR-127's `label-content-name-mismatch`, FR-157/162/163's `identical-links-same-purpose`): a check that reads "passing" but was never capable of catching the thing it's named for. Here the stakes are concrete, not hypothetical — the live deploy has in fact predated FR-109's own git_sha reporting for the eleven days since FR-109 shipped (2026-10-02), while FR-100 (fastapi/starlette CVE fix), FR-117 (pyjwt CVEs), and FR-144 (offline crew-edit data-loss fix) all merged with "backend changed: deploy needed" in that same window — this check has been reporting green on every one of those daily runs without ever actually comparing a SHA. Checked for duplicates first: `scripts/check_backlog_duplicates.py --skip-prs` top matches 50% (FR-162/163, the axe-core rules this item's `why` explicitly draws the same lesson from, not a repeat of that work) and 31% (FR-109 itself, this item's own named follow-up). `python scripts/check_company_docs.py docs/company` clean.
- dod:
  - `check()`'s `Result` (and `deploy_matches_main`/`deploy_staleness_message`) distinguish three states, not two: verified up to date, verified behind, and cannot verify (no `git_sha` yet, or the GitHub compare API itself failed) — the "cannot verify" state must not be representable as the same value as a real pass
  - the markdown table renderer in `check_live_stack.py` prints a visually distinct marker for "cannot verify" (e.g. `⚠️`) instead of `✅`, and the job does not fail the workflow on this state alone (still exit 0 — a missing SHA is not itself a live-stack outage)
  - `scripts/tests/test_check_live_stack.py` gets a case asserting the cannot-verify state renders as the new marker, not `✅`, and that it does not flip the script's overall exit code
  - update `docs/research/pilot-scale-2026-09.md` or wherever this check's own history is recorded to note the gap and its fix, same as FR-162/163 did for `axe-ci-gate-2026-10.md`
  - failure case: if making "cannot verify" fail the job outright turns out to be the only way to make it visually distinct (i.e. a three-state renderer is rejected as infeasible), stop and say so in the doc rather than silently leaving it `✅`, and leave the state as a loud `❌` with the real reason in the detail column instead of a false pass — never ship a middle ground that still prints `✅`

Found this item instead by reading the live "pilot readiness" GitHub Actions run directly rather than re-running an already-exhausted desk-research angle.

## FR-172 · qa · Confirm `check_company_docs.py`'s own CI step ("Company docs format") actually fails on a real malformed `BACKLOG.md`/`INBOX.md` item, not just in local pytest (FR-170/171 pattern, one gate they did not cover)
- state: done
- gated: no
- why: `ci.yml`'s "Company docs format" step (`python scripts/check_company_docs.py docs/company`) runs unconditionally on every PR — unlike the `RUN_HEAVY`-gated Lighthouse/PDF/PWA checks, it is not even skippable on a docs-only PR — and gates every single merge to `main`, this routine's own state/backlog/research PRs included. `scripts/tests/test_check_company_docs.py` exercises `check_backlog`/`check_inbox` as pure functions locally, the same shape FR-162 (axe-core) and FR-170 (`check_conventions.py`) found insufficient on its own: a real wiring gap (a step that silently doesn't run, or a shell quoting issue that swallows its own exit code) only shows up when the hosted `ci` check is actually watched turn red on a real PR. FR-170/FR-171 confirmed exactly this end to end for the branch/title and migration-head gates this same day; neither of their own notes, nor the 15:11 shift's closing claim that "every `ci.yml` gate... has now been exercised end to end," named this step — it was outside the four gates (`FR-162`/`FR-164`/`FR-166`/`FR-170`/`FR-171`) that audit explicitly enumerated, so it is a genuinely unconfirmed gate, not a re-check of one already done.
- dod:
  - on a throwaway branch (same unmerged-until-closed precedent as FR-170/FR-171), introduce one genuine violation `check_company_docs.py` is built to catch — e.g. a `BACKLOG.md` item with `state: redy` (a typo, not one of the five allowed values) or an `INBOX.md` item missing its `risk:` field — open a real PR against `main` and watch the hosted `ci` job's "Company docs format" step actually print the problem and exit non-zero (read the run log directly, not inferred from source)
  - confirm via `pull_request_read get` (or equivalent) that the PR's `mergeable_state` is actually blocked while that check is red, the same direct confirmation FR-170/FR-171 used instead of trusting the check name alone
  - close the throwaway PR without merging as soon as the red check is observed and recorded, and attempt to delete the remote throwaway branch (may 403, as it has for FR-170/FR-171 — harmless either way since the PR stays closed)
  - record the confirmed result in a new dated section of `docs/research/ci-gate-conventions-2026-10.md` (the same file FR-170/FR-171 used, already scoped to "CI gates beyond axe-core")
  - failure case: if the hosted check does *not* turn red on a genuine malformed item (e.g. the step's shell invocation swallows a non-zero exit, or the step is accidentally skipped for some PR shapes), say so plainly, do not file it as done-and-fine, and leave a `tech-lead` backlog item naming the exact gap found rather than guessing a fix in this item
